### Claude · 2026-09-28 16:29
Üç araştırma da bitti. Her şey salt-okunur yapıldı, hiçbir dosya silinmedi ya da taşınmadı. Belge envanterini dosyaya yazdım:
- **Özet ve topluluklar:** `UXBc\claude_arsiv\raporlar\UXBASIC_BELGE_ENVANTERI_20260928.md`
- **315 belgenin tam listesi:** aynı klasörde `...ENVANTERI_20260928.csv` (Excel ile açılır; amaç, başlık, yol, tarih, eski sürüm sayısı)

## uXBasic belgeleri: ne buldum

Bilgisayarda uXBasic'le ilgili **53.898 belge ya da metin** var. Kopyalar ayıklanınca yığının kaynağı belli oluyor:
- **~18.000:** `uxb\reports` altındaki üretilmiş rapor kopyaları.
- **~3.000:** `tekdosyaklsoru` klasörü. Kaynak kodun txt'ye dökülmüş hali, belge değil.
- **~5.000:** `dist\_stage` ve `release_packages` dağıtım kopyaları, `Downloads\UXM XMX` ve `Downloads\1\UXMv33` eski çalışma kopyaları.

**Gerçek, benzersiz belge sayısı yalnızca 315.** Amaçlarına göre:

| Amaç | Sayı | Asıl yer |
|---|---|---|
| Tasarım / mimari / karar | 86 | `uxb\docs\tasarim`, `uxb\planyap`, `uxb\spec` |
| Dil kılavuzu / başvuru | 78 | `uxb\docs`, `Downloads\` (kılavuzların bazı sürümleri burada kök dizinde duruyor) |
| Koordinasyon / sorun takibi | 37 | `uxb\ai_koordinasyon` (sorun defteri, Claude↔Codex durumu, OpenClaw günlüğü) |
| RAG / ajan / araç | 31 | `UxbRAG\...V4`, `UxbRAG\v5`, `UXB_CONTROL_CENTER` |
| Sürüm / dağıtım | 20 | `uxb\tools\uxotcc_final_release`, `UXBCcc\optional_bridge` |
| Denetim / rapor | 20 | `UXBc\` kök dizini (mimari analiz, eksik kontrol raporu) |
| Kitap / öğretici | 10 | `UXBc\`, `UxbRAG\...\kaynaklar\uxbasic_pck_kitaplar` |
| Kütüphane belgesi | 5 | `uxb\docs` (SDL3 kılavuzu vb.) |
| Oyun belgeleri | ~17 | `uxb\game\uxstar`, `uxb\game\startrek` |

**Dikkat çeken dağınıklıklar:**
- **Kılavuzların birden çok sürümü `Downloads` kökünde duruyor**, projeden ayrı: "uXBasic Hedeflenen Sistem ve Kullanma Kılavuzu", "Profesyonel Kullanma Kılavuzu (WASM)", tarihli sürümleri. Proje içindeki `uxb\docs` ile hangisinin güncel olduğu belirsiz.
- **Eski bir Nisan 2026 yedeği** var: `Backups\BasicOyunSource_workspace_git_20260414`. İçindeki 9 belge (Fazlı Operasyon Planı, UBASIC→Windows 11 sınıf planı) başka yerde yok, yani benzersiz.
- **EditPad eklenti kiti:** `UXBc\editpad eklentisi\` (renklendirme ve katlama reçeteleri). VS Code eklentisi için iyi bir başlangıç kaynağı.

## Disk (önceki mesajın özeti)
En büyük iki kazanç sende: **Geri Dönüşüm Kutusu 31 GB** ve **`OneDrive\Documents\pdsx-cursor\interpreter_results.log` 27.8 GB** (kontrolden çıkmış tek bir log dosyası). İkisiyle ~59 GB açılır. Onayınla ben de ~9.4 GB'lık yeniden oluşturulabilir önbelleği temizleyebilirim (npm, uv, Playwright).

## Yüklü programlar
70 masaüstü programı ve Store uygulamaları var. Kullanmıyorsan kaldırmaya aday büyükler:
- Power BI Desktop 2.7 GB
- Power Automate Desktop 1.2 GB
- IBM SPSS 1.3 GB
- Anaconda 10.9 GB (Python 3.12 ayrıca kurulu)
- üç ayrı veri kurtarma programı: 4DDiG, Wise Data Recovery, TestDisk

Program kaldırmayı sen yapmalısın: **Ayarlar → Uygulamalar → Yüklü uygulamalar**.

**Önerim:** Envanterdeki "Dil kılavuzu" grubundaki 78 belge, yerel uzmanın "dil kartı" için ana kaynak olacak. İstersen bir sonraki adımda bunları okuyup güncel ve çelişkisiz tek bir dil kartında birleştirebilirim. Eski sürümler de o sırada `claude_arsiv\eski_belgeler`'e taşınır.

| **3. Programlama rehberi + kütüphaneler** | `uxbasic_resmi_teknik_kitap_12_06_1 (2).md`, **107 bin kelime**, 12 Haziran (`github_publish\...\docs_build\`) | En kapsamlısı: AST/MIR/JS/WASM/x64 dahil 20 bölüm. 2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - uXBasic compiler (FreeBASIC .fbs) with 4 engines: AST interpreter, MIR interpreter, MIR-based native x64 (NASM), legacy AST-x64. Also JS/WASM.
   - BYREF true aliasing:
     - AST: `ExecVar.refIdxPlus1`.
     - MIR: `UXBMIRInterpVar.aliasOf` cell pointer.
   - Local shadowing:
     - native: `MIRX64FindGlobalSlot` skips the current function's params/locals;
     - MIR: the bridge emits a `MIR_LOCAL_DECL` per routine local.
   - YamaMotoru 4.0.1 emir syntax:
     - directives `#P(dir)`, `#MODE(STRICT|NORMALIZED)`;
     - `#B(file) => #( ref )# #[ new ]#`;
     - `#M(file) => #{ }#`;
     - anchored `#( ... $n.i.m$ ... )# file #+[ ]+` / `#-[ ]-`;
     - flags `--dry-run`, `--apply --yes`, `--rollback-last`; transaction history in `history_v4`.

### Claude · 2026-09-28 18:02
Kısaca: matris sistemi gerçekten bozuk, dosya yazmıyor. Onun yerine kaynağı kendim tarayan bir betik yazdım. Gerçek yüzey belgelerin anlattığından çok daha büyük çıktı.

**`mtx` neden çalışmıyor (S-170 olarak kaydettim)**
- Komut, dosyaları nereye yazacağını yalnız `--out-dir` seçeneğinden okuyor. Ama derleyicinin seçenek listesinde bu seçenek yok; verince "Bilinmeyen seçenek" diyor.
- Seçenek hiç verilmezse varsayılan klasör boş kalıyor ve dosyalar `C:\` köküne yazılmaya çalışılıyor. Windows buna izin vermiyor, hata da denetlenmediği için sessizce geçiliyor.
- Yine de "PASS: 5 dosya üretildi" yazıyor. Kontrol diske yazılan dosyalara değil, bellekteki tablolara bakıyor.
- Düzeltme derleyici kodunda gerekiyor. Kitap işini bekletmemek için şimdilik dokunmadım.

**Yeni tarayıcı: [tools/kitap/yuzey_tara.py](tools/kitap/yuzey_tara.py)**
Her adın hangi katmanda (lexer, parser, AST, MIR, native x64, legacy x64, JS, WASM) geçtiğini ve kaç test ya da örnek programda kullanıldığını çıkarıyor. Sonuçlar `docs/kitaplar/_ortak/` klasöründe:

| Ne | Sayı | Not |
|---|---|---|
| Anahtar kelime | **241** | Önce 173 demiştim, yanlışmış. 10 tanesi hiçbir testte geçmiyor. |
| Yerleşik fonksiyon/komut adayı | **167** | `INSTR`, `LEFT$`, `DICTGET`, `LISTADD`, `ATOMIC`… 53'ü hiç test edilmemiş. |
| Derleyicinin iç adları | 660 | Kullanıcı bunları yazmaz; sistem programlama kitabında (2) anlatılacak. |
| Kütüphane | **46** | Toplam **2422 fonksiyon/sub** ve 539 sabit. 1682 fonksiyon testlerde çağrılıyor. |

Kütüphane tablosu her fonksiyonun parametrelerini, dönüş tipini ve kaynaktaki yerini de veriyor. Test boşluğu en büyük olanlar:
- uxcollections: 210 fonksiyonun 29'u testte çağrılıyor.
- uxgsl: 236'nın 50'si.
- uxterminal: 17'nin 2'si.

Bu eksikler kitaplardaki örneklerle kapanabilir.

**Sıradaki adım:** kopya kağıdı (kitap 5). Lexer anahtar kelimeleri zaten 21 grup halinde tutuyor: akış, hata yakalama, EVENT/THREAD/PIPE/SLOT, döngüler, tipler, erişim, MAIN/NAMESPACE/MODULE, operatörler, bellek komutları ve diğerleri. İskeleti bu gruplardan kuracağım. Her komuta bir örnek yazıp üç motorda deneyeceğim; tabloya yalnız çalışan örnekler girecek.

### Mete · 2026-09-28 20:01
# Workflow authoring reference

A workflow structures work across many agents — to be comprehensive (decompose and cover in parallel), to be confident (independent perspectives and adversarial checks before committing), or to take on scale one context can't hold (migrations, audits, broad sweeps). The script is where you encode that structure: what fans out, what verifies, what synthesizes.

When you do call it, the right move is often **hybrid**: scout inline first (list the files, find the channels, scope the diff) to discover the work-list, then call Workflow to pipeline over it. You don't need to know the shape before the *task* — only before the *orchestration step*.

Common single-phase workflows you can chain across turns:
- **Understand** — parallel readers over relevant subsystems → structured map
- **Design** — judge panel of N independent approaches → scored synthesis
- **Review** — dimensions → find → adversarially verify (the review-changes example)
- **Research** — multi-modal sweep → deep-read → synthesize
- **Migrate** — discover sites → transform each (worktree isolation) → verify

For larger work, run several in sequence — read each result before deciding the next phase. You stay in the loop; each workflow is one well-scoped fan-out.

**Ultracode.** When a system-reminder confirms ultracode is on, that opt-in is standing: author and run a workflow for every substantive task by default. The goal is the most exhaustive, correct answer you can produce — token cost is not a constraint. For multi-phase work (understand → design → implement → review), that often means several workflows in sequence — one per phase — so you stay in the loop between them. The quality patterns below (adversarial verify, multi-modal sweep, completeness critic, loop-until-dry) are the tools; pick what fits the task. Lean toward orchestrating with workflows and adversarially verifying your findings — unless the work is trivial or already verified. Solo only on conversational turns or trivial mechanical edits. When a reminder says ultracode is off, revert to the opt-in rule in the Workflow tool description.

Pass the script inline via `script` — do not Write it to a file first. Every invocation automatically persists its script to a file under the session directory and returns the path in the tool result. To iterate on a workflow, edit that file with Write/Edit and re-invoke Workflow with `{scriptPath: "<path>"}` instead of resending the full script.

Every script must begin with `export const meta = {...}`:
  export const meta = {
    name: 'find-flaky-tests',
    description: 'Find flaky tests and propose fixes',   // one-line, shown in permission dialog
    phases: [                                            // one entry per phase() call
      { title: 'Scan', detail: 'grep test logs for retries' },
      { title: 'Fix', detail: 'one agent per flaky test' },
    ],
  }
  // script body starts here — use agent()/parallel()/pipeline()/phase()/log()
  phase('Scan')
  const flaky = await agent('grep CI logs for retry markers', {schema: FLAKY_SCHEMA})
  ...

The `meta` object must be a PURE LITERAL — no variables, function calls, spreads, or template interpolation. Required fields: `name`, `description`. Optional: `whenToUse` (shown in the workflow list), `phases`. Use the SAME phase titles in meta.phases as in phase() calls — titles are matched exactly; a phase() call with no matching meta entry just gets its own progress group. Add `model` to a phase entry when that phase uses a specific model override.

Script body hooks:
- agent(prompt: string, opts?: {label?: string, phase?: string, schema?: object, model?: string, effort?: string, isolation?: 'worktree', agentType?: string}): Promise<any> — spawn a subagent. Without schema, returns its final text as a string. With schema (a JSON Schema), the subagent is forced to call a StructuredOutput tool and agent() returns the validated object — no parsing needed. Returns null if the user skips the agent mid-run or the subagent dies on a terminal API error after retries (filter with .filter(Boolean)). opts.label overrides the display label. opts.phase explicitly assigns this agent to a progress group (use this inside pipeline()/parallel() stages to avoid races on the global phase() state — same phase string → same group box). opts.model overrides the model for this agent call. Default to omitting it — the agent inherits the main-loop model (the resolved session model), which is almost always correct. Only set it when you're highly confident a different tier fits the task; when unsure, omit. opts.effort overrides the reasoning effort for this agent call ('low' | 'medium' | 'high' | 'xhigh' | 'max') — omit to inherit the session effort; use 'low' for cheap mechanical stages and higher tiers only for the hardest verify/judge stages. opts.isolation: 'worktree' runs the agent in a fresh git worktree — EXPENSIVE (~200-500ms setup + disk per agent), use ONLY when agents mutate files in parallel and would otherwise conflict; the worktree is auto-removed if unchanged. opts.agentType uses a custom subagent type (e.g. 'general-purpose', 'code-reviewer') instead of the default workflow subagent — resolved from the same registry as the Agent tool; composes with schema (the custom agent's system prompt gets a StructuredOutput instruction appended).
- pipeline(items, stage1, stage2, ...): Promise<any[]> — run each item through all stages independently, NO barrier between stages. Item A can be in stage 3 while item B is still in stage 1. This is the DEFAULT for multi-stage work. Wall-clock = slowest single-item chain, not sum-of-slowest-per-stage. Every stage callback receives (prevResult, originalItem, index) — use originalItem/index in later stages to label work without threading context through stage 1's return value. A stage that throws drops that item to `null` and skips its remaining stages.
- parallel(thunks: Array<() => Promise<any>>): Promise<any[]> — run tasks concurrently. This is a BARRIER: awaits all thunks before returning. A thunk that throws (or whose agent errors) resolves to `null` in the result array — the call itself never rejects, so `.filter(Boolean)` before using the results. Use ONLY when you genuinely need all results together.
- log(message: string): void — emit a progress message to the user (shown as a narrator line above the progress tree)
- phase(title: string): void — start a new phase; subsequent agent() calls are grouped under this title in the progress display
- args: any — the value passed as Workflow's `args` input, verbatim (undefined if not provided). Pass arrays/objects as actual JSON values in the tool call, NOT as a JSON-encoded string — `args: ["a.ts", "b.ts"]`, not `args: "[\"a.ts\", ...]"` (a stringified list reaches the script as one string, so `args.filter`/`args.map` throw). Use this to parameterize named workflows — e.g. pass a research question, target path, or config object directly instead of via a side-channel file.
- budget: {total: number|null, spent(): number, remaining(): number} — the turn's token target from the user's "+500k"-style directive. `budget.total` is null if no target was set. `budget.spent()` returns output tokens spent this turn across the main loop and all workflows — the pool is shared, not per-workflow. `budget.remaining()` returns `max(0, total - spent())`, or `Infinity` if no target. The target is a HARD ceiling, not advisory: once `spent()` reaches `total`, further `agent()` calls throw. Use for dynamic loops: `while (budget.total && budget.remaining() > 50_000) { ... }`, or static scaling: `const FLEET = budget.total ? Math.floor(budget.total / 100_000) : 5`.
- workflow(nameOrRef: string | {scriptPath: string}, args?: any): Promise<any> — run another workflow inline as a sub-step and return whatever it returns. Pass a name to invoke a saved workflow (same registry as {name: "..."}), or {scriptPath} to run a script file you Wrote earlier. The child shares this run's concurrency cap, agent counter, abort signal, and token budget — its agents appear under a "▸ name" group in /workflows and its tokens count toward budget.spent(). The args param becomes the child's `args` global. Nesting is one level only: workflow() inside a child throws. Throws on unknown name / unreadable scriptPath / child syntax error; catch to handle gracefully.

Subagents are told their final text IS the return value (not a human-facing message), so they return raw data. For structured output, use the schema option — validation happens at the tool-call layer so the model retries on mismatch.
Schemas need {type: 'object', properties: {...}} at root and required ⊆ properties; unsatisfiable ones throw at agent().

Workflow agents can reach all session-connected MCP tools via ToolSearch — schemas load on demand per agent. Caveat: interactively-authenticated MCP servers (e.g. claude.ai) may be absent in headless/cron runs.

Subagents get the same CLAUDE.md files injected at start that you did (except built-in agent types that omit them, such as Explore and Plan) — don't tell them to re-read those or paste their rules into the prompt; name the specific rule a stage needs, if any.

Scripts are plain JavaScript, NOT TypeScript — type annotations (`: string[]`), interfaces, and generics fail to parse. The script body runs in an async context — use await directly. Standard JS built-ins (JSON, Math, Array, etc.) are available — EXCEPT `Date.now()`/`Math.random()`/argless `new Date()`, which throw (they would break resume); pass timestamps in via `args`, stamp results after the workflow returns, and for randomness vary the agent prompt/label by index. No filesystem or Node.js API access.

DEFAULT TO pipeline(). Only reach for a barrier (parallel between stages) when you genuinely need ALL prior-stage results together.

A barrier is correct ONLY when stage N needs cross-item context from all of stage N-1:
- Dedup/merge across the full result set before expensive downstream work
- Early-exit if the total count is zero ("0 bugs found → skip verification entirely")
- Stage N's prompt references "the other findings" for comparison

A barrier is NOT justified by:
- "I need to flatten/map/filter first" — do it inside a pipeline stage: pipeline(items, stageA, r => transform([r]).flat(), stageB)
- "The stages are conceptually separate" — that's what pipeline() models. Separate stages ≠ synchronized stages.
- "It's cleaner code" — barrier latency is real. If 5 finders run and the slowest takes 3× the fastest, a barrier wastes 2/3 of the fast finders' idle time.

Smell test: if you wrote
  const a = await parallel(...)
  const b = transform(a)        // flatten, map, filter — no cross-item dependency
  const c = await parallel(b.map(...))
that middle transform doesn't need the barrier. Rewrite as a pipeline with the transform inside a stage. When in doubt: pipeline.

Concurrent agent() calls are capped at min(16, available CPUs - 2) per workflow — excess calls queue and run as slots free up. You can still pass 100 items to parallel()/pipeline() and they all complete; only ~10 run at any moment. Total agent count across a workflow's lifetime is capped at 1000 — a runaway-loop backstop set far above any real workflow. A single parallel()/pipeline() call accepts at most 4096 items; passing more is an explicit error, not a silent truncation.

When a barrier IS correct — dedup across all findings before expensive verification:
  const all = await parallel(DIMENSIONS.map(d => () => agent(d.prompt, {schema: FINDINGS_SCHEMA})))
  const deduped = dedupeByFileAndLine(all.filter(Boolean).flatMap(r => r.findings))  // <-- genuinely needs ALL at once
  const verified = await parallel(deduped.map(f => () => agent(verifyPrompt(f), {schema: VERDICT_SCHEMA})))

Loop-until-count pattern — accumulate to a target:
  const bugs = []
  while (bugs.length < 10) {
    const result = await agent("Find bugs in this codebase.", {schema: BUGS_SCHEMA})
    bugs.push(...result.bugs)
    log(`${bugs.length}/10 found`)
  }

Loop-until-budget pattern — scale depth to the user's "+500k" directive. Guard on budget.total: with no target set, remaining() is Infinity and the loop would run straight to the 1000-agent cap.
  const bugs = []
  while (budget.total && budget.remaining() > 50_000) {
    const result = await agent("Find bugs in this codebase.", {schema: BUGS_SCHEMA})
    bugs.push(...result.bugs)
    log(`${bugs.length} found, ${Math.round(budget.remaining()/1000)}k remaining`)
  }

Composing patterns — exhaustive review (find → dedup vs seen → diverse-lens panel → loop-until-dry):
  const seen = new Set(), confirmed = []
  let dry = 0
  while (dry < 2) {                                              // loop-until-dry
    const found = (await parallel(FINDERS.map(f => () =>          // barrier: collect all finders this round
      agent(f.prompt, {phase: 'Find', schema: BUGS})))).filter(Boolean).flatMap(r => r.bugs)
    const fresh = found.filter(b => !seen.has(key(b)))           // dedup vs ALL seen — plain code, not an agent
    if (!fresh.length) { dry++; continue }
    dry = 0; fresh.forEach(b => seen.add(key(b)))
    const judged = await parallel(fresh.map(b => () =>           // every fresh bug judged concurrently...
      parallel(['correctness','security','repro'].map(lens => () =>   // ...each by 3 distinct lenses
        agent(`Judge "${b.desc}" via the ${lens} lens — real?`, {phase: 'Verify', schema: VERDICT})))
        .then(vs => ({ b, real: vs.filter(Boolean).filter(v => v.real).length >= 2 }))))
    confirmed.push(...judged.filter(v => v.real).map(v => v.b))
  }
  return confirmed
  // dedup vs `seen`, NOT `confirmed` — else judge-rejected findings reappear every round and it never converges.

Quality patterns — common shapes; pick by task and compose freely:
- Adversarial verify: spawn N independent skeptics per finding, each prompted to REFUTE. Kill if ≥majority refute. Prevents plausible-but-wrong findings from surviving.
    const votes = await parallel(Array.from({length: 3}, () => () =>
      agent(`Try to refute: ${claim}. Default to refuted=true if uncertain.`, {schema: VERDICT})))
    const survives = votes.filter(Boolean).filter(v => !v.refuted).length >= 2
- Perspective-diverse verify: when a finding can fail in more than one way, give each verifier a distinct lens (correctness, security, perf, does-it-reproduce) instead of N identical refuters — diversity catches failure modes redundancy can't.
- Judge panel: generate N independent attempts from different angles (e.g. MVP-first, risk-first, user-first), score with parallel judges, synthesize from the winner while grafting the best ideas from runners-up. Beats one-attempt-iterated when the solution space is wide.
- Loop-until-dry: for unknown-size discovery (bugs, issues, edge cases), keep spawning finders until K consecutive rounds return nothing new. Simple counters (while count < N) miss the tail.
- Multi-modal sweep: parallel agents each searching a different way (by-container, by-content, by-entity, by-time). Each is blind to what the others surface; useful when one search angle won't find everything.
- Completeness critic: a final agent that asks "what's missing — modality not run, claim unverified, source unread?" What it finds becomes the next round of work.
- No silent caps: if a workflow bounds coverage (top-N, no-retry, sampling), `log()` what was dropped — silent truncation reads as "covered everything" when it didn't.

Scale to what the user asked for. "find any bugs" → a few finders, single-vote verify. "thoroughly audit this" or "be comprehensive" → larger finder pool, 3–5 vote adversarial pass, synthesis stage. When unsure, lean toward thoroughness for research/review/audit requests and toward brevity for quick checks.

These patterns aren't exhaustive — compose novel harnesses when the task calls for it (tournament brackets, self-repair loops, staged escalation, whatever fits).

Use this tool for multi-step orchestration where control flow should be deterministic (loops, conditionals, fan-out) rather than model-driven.

## Resume

The tool result includes a runId. To resume after a pause, kill, or script edit, relaunch with Workflow({scriptPath, resumeFromRunId}) — the longest unchanged prefix of agent() calls returns cached results instantly; the first edited/new call and everything after it runs live. Same script + same args → 100% cache hit. Before diagnosing why a completed workflow returned an empty or unexpected result, Read <transcriptDir>/journal.jsonl — it records each agent's actual return value; do not assume cached results are non-empty. Date.now()/Math.random()/new Date() are unavailable in scripts (they would break this) — stamp results after the workflow returns, or pass timestamps via args. Fallback when no journal is available: Read agent-<id>.jsonl files in the transcript directory and hand-author a continuation script.

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   Build Book 5 (Cheat Sheet/Kopya Kağıdı) for uXBasic language by:
   - Creating a structured example corpus organized by the 21 keyword groups already present in the lexer
   - Writing working examples for each command/feature
   - Testing each example on three execution engines (AST interpreter, MIR interpreter, native x64)
   - Including only examples that pass on all three engines in the final cheat sheet

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - uXBasic: FreeBASIC-based compiler with 4 execution engines (AST interpreter, MIR interpreter, native x64, legacy AST-x64)
   - Language surface: 241 keywords, 167 builtin functions/commands, 46 libraries with 2422 functions total
   - Execution engines vary in behavior; cheat sheet must show unified/consistent examples
   - S-030, S-146, S-167, S-168, S-162, S-087 represent major architectural work (task model, sync pass, BYREF aliasing, routine scope)
   - Git repository exists (MinGit); 854 uncommitted files on main branch; Codex and second Claude are concurrent collaborators

3. Files and Code Sections:

   **tools/kitap/yuzey_tara.py**
   - Surface/language scanner that extracts actual implementation from compiler source
   - Scans .fbs files for Case statements and string comparisons to find where each name is implemented
   - Classifies into: keywords (lexer anahtarı), builtins (yerleşik), internal compiler names (iç)
   - For libraries: parses NAMESPACE, FUNCTION/SUB signatures from libsx/*.bas
   - Outputs: dil_yuzeyi.json (all names with layers), kutuphane_yuzeyi.json (all 46 libraries with function signatures)
   - Reports: 241 keywords, 167 builtins in kernel language; 46 libraries, 2422 functions/subs, 539 constants total

   **tools/kitap/ornek_dogrula.py**
   - Example validator: runs .uxb programs on 3 engines and compares output
   - File format: comment headers with KONU (topic), KOMUTLAR (command list), ACIKLAMA (description), then '= lines for expected output, then code
   - Runs interpreters in temp directory (not project build/); compiles native to temp; copies .dll files
   - Output: ornek_sonuclari.json with per-engine status (GECTI/FARKLI/HATA) and first 40 output lines
   - Multithreaded (default 3 workers)

   **tools/kitap/ornek_uret1.py** (scratchpad, not committed)
   - Generates 51 initial examples covering major language features
   - Organized into groups: 01_akis (flow), 02_hata (errors), 03_olay (events), 04_gorev (tasks), 05_dongu (loops), etc.
   - Each example is minimal (10-30 lines), self-contained, includes explicit expected output
   - Covers: IF/ELSEIF/ELSE, SELECT CASE, GOTO/GOSUB, FOR/WHILE/DO loops, FOR EACH, TRY/CATCH/FINALLY, THROW, ASSERT, SUB/FUNCTION/RETURN, BYREF/BYVAL, recursion, TYPE/CLASS/NEW/METHOD, NAMESPACE/MODULE/USING/MAIN, EVENT/SLOT/TRIGGER, THREAD/SPAWN/TASK/JOIN, LOCK/ATOMIC, POKE/PEEK/MEMFILL/MEMCOPY, arithmetic/comparison/logic operators, string/math functions, LIST/DICT/SET, %%DEFINE/%%IF

   **docs/kitaplar/_ortak/ornek_sonuclari.json**
   - Results database: each example with KONU/KOMUTLAR/ACIKLAMA, beklenen (expected output), motor (AST/MIR/x64 status/output)
   - Currently 71 total entries (51 from batch 1 + 18 probes + 2 earlier); 42/53 from batch 1 pass all 3 engines

   **ai_koordinasyon/SORUN_TAKIP.md**
   - S-170 added: mtx command broken (9 lines describing CLI option missing, file write failure, validation only checks memory)

4. Errors and fixes:
   - **Matrix system (S-170)**: `uxb mtx` reports "5 JSON files produced" but only produces 1 (runtime_route_matrix.json in session output, not main out-dir). Root cause: `--out-dir` option not registered in CLI registry; when absent, GetArgValue falls through, path becomes empty, file writes attempt C:\surface_backend_matrix.json etc. and fail silently. Validation passes because UXBValidateAllSurfaceBackendMatrices checks in-memory generated tables, not disk. Fixed by creating direct source scanner (yuzey_tara.py) instead of relying on mtx.
   
   - **Git author placeholder**: Initial commit shows author as "Adınız" / "email@adresiniz.com". Fixed by setting GIT_AUTHOR_NAME/EMAIL environment variables explicitly to metedinler/[e-posta] before commit.
   
   - **PowerShell commit message handling**: PowerShell parsed multiline commit message as file argument instead of stdin. Fixed by writing message to temp file and using `git commit -F <file>`.
   
   - **Build folder pollution**: Example validator created 6 build\09_28_2026_22_* folders in project root. Moved to %TEMP%\uxb_kitap_eski_build per move-don't-delete policy.
   
   - **Example failures (probe results)**:
     - **SELECT CASE TO bounds**: `CASE 4 TO 6` with n=5 returns "disari" (else) instead of "aralik". All 3 engines fail consistently—likely parser/semantic bug in range handling.
     - **CONTINUE statement**: `CONTINUE FOR` parse error "FOR variable expected". CONTINUE/CONTINUE FOR not implemented; only syntax is `EXIT FOR`/`EXIT DO`/`EXIT IF`.
     - **TRY/CATCH/FINALLY**: Semantic error "unreachable statement after THROW_STMT" when PRINT follows THROW in same block. S-160 related (exception handling not fully working).
     - **Type casting**: `DIM a AS I8 = -128` fails with "assignment requires explicit cast TYPE_I32 to TYPE_I8". Literals treated as I32; need `CAST(I8, -128)` or initialize through intermediate expression.
     - **String functions**: `LEFT$`, `RIGHT$`, `STR$` cause "Lexer diagnostic bridge hata buldu: 2". Possible issue with dollar-sign suffix handling in lexer.
     - **PRINT with comma**: AST produces "1             2" (wide spacing, not comma), MIR/x64 fail with missing PRINT_SEP_COMMA metadata. Separator not fully implemented in MIR code gen.
     - **Hex/Octal/Binary literals**: `&HFF`, `&B101`, `&O17` parse error "statement parse failed near OP('&')". Literal syntax not recognized by parser.
     - **VAL() on native**: `VAL("40")` returns wrong value (1.976e-322) on native x64 but correct on AST/MIR. Type coercion bug in native FFI.
     - **SELECT CASE string (AST only)**: `ad = "elma"; CASE "elma", "armut"` works on MIR/x64 but AST matches CASE ELSE. S-042/S-043 regression or AST-specific bug.
     - **MEMCOPY byte order**: Last byte copied becomes 0 instead of 9 on all engines. Possible off-by-one in MEMCOPYB loop.
     - **DIM in loop**: AST reports "duplicate variable X" on second loop iteration; MIR/x64 correctly allow redeclaration in new loop scope. S-030 scoping issue.
     - **Float precision**: 10/3 produces 3.333333333333334 (AST/MIR), 3.333333333333333 (x64). Rounding differences; x64 uses different FPU precision.

5. Problem Solving:
   - **Matrix system broken**: Investigated CLI option registry, found `--out-dir` not declared. Determined file writes fail silently, validation only checks memory. Solution: implemented `yuzey_tara.py` direct source scanner as workaround, producing equivalent surface documentation from compiler source.
   - **Large probe failure set**: Out of 71 examples, 29 fail (11 failing in batch 1, 18 in probes). Failures span multiple areas: not-implemented features (CONTINUE), incomplete implementations (TRY/CATCH, PRINT comma), parser gaps (hex literals, dollar sign), and engine inconsistencies (SELECT CASE string, float precision, MEMCOPY). Strategy: keep working examples in final cheat sheet, file bugs for failures (S-170 already filed; others would map to S-160, S-030, etc.).
   - **Concurrent development constraints**: Codex and second Claude actively working on this repo; memory notes indicate check mtimes, avoid game/ folder, never touch bin\uxb.exe. Commit successful with explicit author to distinguish from Codex's work.

Summary:
## 1. Primary Request and Intent

Mete requested the creation of **ONE canonical uXBasic Language Definition (Dil Tanımı)** document that:
- Specifies every command, function, operator, data type, data structure, and library working per its design
- Is backward compatible with previous version
- Contains NO placeholders, fake code, or "it works" claims without verification on actual test runs
- Covers all 3 execution engines (AST interpreter, MIR interpreter, native x64) producing identical observable behavior
- Collapses redundant command variants into single canonical forms
- Is organized around 21 keyword groups already present in the lexer

Mete emphasized: "Her komuta bir örnek yazıp üç motorda deneyeceğim; tabloya yalnız çalışan örnekler girecek" (Write an example for each command, test on three engines; include only working examples in the table).

He also requested visible status reporting: "Bu belirsizlik beni belkide bitmis bir urunu devamli gelistirmeye zorluyor" - he cannot see whether systems (map fill, watcher, spec writing) are running or where to interact.

## 2. Key Technical Concepts

- **Type System**: I8 U8 I16 U16 I32 U32 I64 U64 F32 F64 F80 F128 BIGF BIGD BALL BOOLEAN STRING PTR (NOT LONG/INTEGER/DOUBLE; NO $ # % ! suffixes)
- **Explicit Conversion**: Canonical form is type-name functions: `I8(x)`, `U8(x)`, `I32(x)`, `F32(x)` - verified working on all 3 engines (Mete corrected me on this)
- **Three Execution Engines**: AST interpreter, MIR interpreter, native x64 (NASM) - must produce identical results ("motor eşitliği")
- **Lexer Keyword Groups**: 21 groups already organized in lexer_keyword_table.fbs (akış, hata, EVENT/THREAD, döngüler, tipler, erişim, MAIN/NAMESPACE, operatörler, bellek, etc.)
- **Example Validation Pipeline**: ornek_dogrula.py runs .uxb files on 3 engines with expected output checking (60s timeout, 2MB output cap to prevent runaway loops)
- **Language Specification Structure**: docs/kitaplar/_ortak/dil_tanimi/alanlar/<kod>/{bolum.md, ogeler.json, ornekler/*.uxb, sonuc.json, denetim.md, codex/} per area
- **Multi-Model Audit**: Kimi/Gemini/ChatGPT review bölüm chapters for accuracy and completeness
- **Motor Constraints**: S-171…S-190 document 20 known failure categories (not-implemented features, engine inconsistencies, parser gaps)

## 🎯 ÖZET BULGULAR

### ✅ KURULU & ÇALIŞIYOR:
- **Harita Doldurma** — PID 5160, bekçi PID 19300 (otomatik devam etme kurulu)
- **Ollama** — v0.34.4, 8 model yüklü, daemon çalışıyor, API canlı
- **Otomatik Başlatma** — Startup klasöründe kurulu (bilgisayar başlarken çalışır)
- **API Anahtarları** — Ayarlı (.uxb\api_anahtarlari.env)

### ❌ EKSİKLİ:
1. **Sınıf/Metod/Miras Analizi** — Sadece fonksiyon isimleri taranıyor
   - `uxb_symbol_scan.py` → anahtar kelimeler
   - `paketleyici3.py` → fonksiyon amaçları
   - ❌ Sınıf tanımları: `class Foo:`
   - ❌ Metodlar: `def method(self):`
   - ❌ Miras: `class Child(Parent):`

2. **Eğitim Döngüsü** — Ollama kurulu ama özelleştirilmemiş
   - OllamaAsistan.py = manuel asistan (eğitim yok)
   - Fine-tune script'i yok
   - uXBasic'e özel model yok

3. **Kütüphane Yapısı** — Modül analizı yok
   - Kütüphane versiyonları
   - Bağımlılık haritası
   - Döngüsel ilişkiler

### 📋  27 PROGRAM DÖRT KATEGORIYE AYRILIYOR:

**A. Tarama (Symbol Scan, Paketleme)**
- uxb_symbol_scan.py — fonksiyon/keyword taraması
- paketleyici3.py — "GELİŞİMSEL ANALİZÖR" (sürüm 3.0, Mete+Gemini)

**B. Harita Yönetimi**
- uxb_harita_doldur.py — dış LLM'lere soru sorma (✨ ÇALIŞIYOR)
- uxb_map_system.py — veritabanı
- uxb_map_lookup.py — arama

**C. Vektör & Arama**
- uxb_chroma_index.py — embedding
- uxb_chroma_search.py — semantic arama
- uxb_hybrid_ask.py — keyword + semantic karma

**D. Model & Orchestration**
- uxb_ollama.py — Ollama wrapper
- uxb_model_select.py — model seçme
- uxb_pipeline_runner.py — tam pipeline

---

## 🚀 ÇALIŞMA KOMUTLARI

**Şimdi hemen çalıştırabileceklerin:**

```powershell
# 1. Durum
pwsh -NoExit -File DURUM_GOSTER.ps1

# 2. Harita doldur (Kimi ile, Gemini kota doldu)
cd UxbRAG\UXB_HYBRID_RAG_V4_YORUMLU_JSON
python tools/uxb_harita_doldur.py --is amac --max-istek 5 --saglayici kimi

# 3. Paket yenile
python tools/uxb_ajan_paketi.py

# 4. Sembol index
python tools/uxb_symbol_scan.py --config config.json

# 5. Tam pipeline (otomatik hepsi)
python tools/uxb_pipeline_runner.py --pipeline full --workers 2
```

- ✓ **Ollama** — 8 model kurulu, qwen2.5-coder:3b eğitim için
- ✓ **Amaç üreticisi** — 166 rutin/30 dakika (Ollama, 20+ saat süre)
- ✓ **Ajan paketi** — Claude/Codex okuyabiliyor
- ✓ **Paketleyici3** — FBS kodlarını paket olarak verdi
- ✓ **Otomatik başlatma** — Bilgisayar başlangıcında çalışıyor
- ✓ **Kodex Moduller (17 dosya)** — StatikAnalizator, DavranisAnalizor, RAGVeriHazirlayici
- ✓ **Token tabanlı sistem** — (mimarı yazıldı)

### 🚧 Yazılacak (Sıra):
1. **`uxb_groq.py`** — Groq API wrapper (ücretsiz LLM, fallback)
2. **`uxb_token_manager.py`** — Dünkü/günlük/toplam tüketim raporu
3. **`uxb_class_analyzer.py`** — TYPE/CLASS hiyerarşi (Mete'nin önceki araştırması var mı?)
4. **`uxb_hybrid_llm.py`** — Fallback chaining (Ollama → Groq → Gemini → Claude)
5. **Fine-tune dataset** — qwen2.5-coder için training data hazırlığı

2. **`.env` dosyasını bul** — tek kaynak, oradan oku

```powershell
# .env dosyasını bul
Get-ChildItem -Path "$env:USERPROFILE" -Recurse -Name "*.env" -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Where-Object { $_ -match 'api|credential|key' }

# Ya da spesifik yerleri ara
Test-Path "$env:USERPROFILE\.env"
Test-Path "$env:USERPROFILE\.uxb\api_anahtarlari.env"
Test-Path "C:\Users\mete\.omniroute\.env"
```

**Bulduğunda, yolunu yaz.** Sonra `uxb_groq.py`'i güncellerim (model + .env okuma).

(`uxb_groq.py`'de şu kodu eklerim:)
```python
from dotenv import load_dotenv

# .env bul ve yükle
env_files = [
    os.path.expanduser('~/.env'),
    os.path.expanduser('~/.uxb/api_anahtarlari.env'),
    '.env'
]
for env_file in env_files:
    if os.path.exists(env_file):
        load_dotenv(env_file)
        break
```

`.env` dosyasının tam yolunu ver.

✅ **Dosyalar bulundu. Bridge MVP başlatılıyor:**

```powershell
# Native host başlat
cd C:\Users\mete\Downloads\BasicOyunSource\UXBc_Clean\UXBc\mete_prompt_bridge_mvp

```

**Opera'da şunları yap:**
1. **LLM sayfasına git** (OpenAI/Claude/Gemini)
2. **Eklenti panelini aç** (sağ üst)
3. **Sorgu yazıp gönder**
4. **Sonuç txt dosyasını kontrol et:** `~/.uxb/emirler/completed/`

**Başarı kriteri:** 
- ✅ Native host → Opera eklentisine bağlansın
- ✅ prompt_bot → sorguyu `waiting/` klasörüne yaza
- ✅ Token Manager → tüketimi kayda geçir
- ✅ Sonuç → `completed/` klasörüne

**Çalıştırıp sonuçları yolla!**
